Delegating to `_cfg` rather than re-reading the board is the right call: `_cfg` is per-sym > TEMPLATE cat_side > global PLUS three exclusions that must survive (`SUB15M_BLOCKED_SWITCHES`, the always-True exchange-resting-stop keys, `_LIVE_AUTHORITY_TR`). **Measured on `AAPL_BF_LONG`: 3,451 of 3,456 board keys now resolve to the board value; the 5 that do not (`DC_BASIS_3M_REDUCE_ENABLED`, `OBLIGATORY_HEDGE_WT_USE_3M`, `RULE_B_3M_EXIT_ENABLED`, `RULE_B_5M_EXIT_ENABLED`, `WT_CROSSUNDER_FINAL_ENABLED`) are all in `SUB15M_BLOCKED_SWITCHES` (19 keys) — the intended exclusion, not a miss.** The recursion trap (`_cfg` ends `:7854` `return getattr(config, param, default)`) is handled by a `threading.local` re-entrancy guard set in `try` / reset in `finally`; `_cfg` is a plain `def`, so no await can interleave and the guard cannot leak between tasks. Tested: no recursion, no hang.

  **OPEN DEFECT — THE CONTEXT IS NEVER CLEARED. `_tr_psym_ctx_clear` (`:7890`) is defined and called NOWHERE.** The author's argument is that each position runs in its own asyncio Task, which gets a copy of the context, so no reset is needed. That holds for the `asyncio.gather` callers (`:26088`, `:26220`, `:54677`, and `backtest_v12_engine.py:12866`) — gather wraps coroutines in Tasks. **It does NOT hold for `:13793`, `result = await process_position(...)`, inside `monitor_entries`'s `for position_key in batch:` loop (`:13734`): a direct `await` runs in the CALLER's context, so no copy is made.** `monitor_entries` is itself directly awaited from 5 sites (`:11708`, `:26263`, `:26550`, `:54397`, `:54439`), the live periodic loops. So when `monitor_entries` returns, the calling task still carries the LAST processed `(account, symbol, side)`, and every later UPPERCASE `config.X` read in that loop resolves against that one symbol's board instead of the intended layer. Also inconsistent: an ABSENT key raises `AttributeError` with no context but returns **`None`** with a context set (`getattr(inner, name, None)` is passed as `_cfg`'s default), turning a typo'd switch from a loud crash into a silent falsy default on a live decision path.

  **FIX SHAPE (one change, contained):** capture the token — `tok = _tr_psym_ctx_set(...)` — and `_tr_psym_ctx_clear(tok)` in a `finally` around `process_position`'s body, which is exactly what the unused helper was written for; and pass a sentinel rather than `None` as `_cfg`'s default so an absent key still raises. **INERT UNTIL `tradier_manage` RESTARTS** — the market gate restarts it at the next open, so the defect reaches real money then unless fixed first. **Instant kill switch: `TR_PER_SYM_PROXY=0`** reverts to the unwrapped object (i.e. back to the known global leak). NOT pushed to S1; no `v15_version_gate submit` has run.

- **2026-10-18 04:2x–04:5xZ (USER "ONE SIMPLE AND CLEAR RULE while we figure this out: Trade what you promised nothing more nothing less YOU NEED TO MAKE THIS HAPPEN RIGHT NOW") — PROMISE RULE, ENFORCED AND MEASURED. NEW `promise_ledger.py`, NEW `tools/promise_audit.py`, NEW `tests/test_promise_exit_preempt.py`; edited `tools/vx_tracked_exec.py` (not in the lock table) and `live_twins/vec_exact.py` (promise recording only). NO locked file edited, NO config default flipped, NO engine file touched.**

  **MEASURED FIRST, THEN FIXED — "nothing more" was already true; "nothing less" was not.** Of 342 live round trips the 7-day retrace could not match to a vector trip, **340 were opened on a vector-tagged reason** (`|VEC_EXACT`: VEC_CONVERGE_OPEN 122, REENTRY_HIGHER_LOW 89, B_KZONE 25, B12 19 ...) and the only 2 non-vec rows were `BROKER_RECONCILED` bookkeeping - live is not inventing trades. The loss sits on the exit side: of 416 matched trips only **26% exited on the vector's bar** (+10% intrabar stop), **212 were late**, some by 17-206 bars (4 h - 2 days) while the vector cycled in and out up to 13 times, and the single largest late-exit family was `VEC_STALE_FLATTEN` - the hours-later backstop - at **23 trips / -13.31 $** over 7 days.

  **ROOT CAUSE (measured in the live log, not inferred).** `tools/vx_tracked_exec.dispatch_tracked` keys its single-flight `INFLIGHT` map on the **position_key alone**, with no notion of what is running. A vec full-close therefore queued behind ANY slower execution on that key (maker chase, augment) and every retry was answered `SKIPPED_VX_INFLIGHT`: `fin:RLCUSDT_SHORT` 2026-10-18 04:26:22 -> 04:29:32, skipped every ~20 s, cleared only when the broker happened to go flat; today's counts were `VEC_EXACT_EXIT_OWED` 79 (fin) / 19 (flz) / 17 (men). The promise was made at bar b and delivered hours later by a watchdog.

  **THE FIX (one chokepoint, two rules, kill switches).** (1) **An exit may never wait behind a non-exit**: an incoming CLOSE/REDUCE dispatches immediately while a non-exit keeps shepherding its own order (`EXIT_PREEMPTS_INFLIGHT`, env `VX_EXIT_PREEMPT=0` restores the old behaviour); the superseded task is deliberately NOT cancelled (2026-10-13 stranding lesson) so its order stays managed - if it still fills, live holds a small position the vector does not, and the next-bar close is no longer suppressed either. (2) **A wedged exit escalates instead of being skipped forever**: a second exit arriving while an in-flight exit is older than `VX_EXIT_FORCE_AFTER_S` (90 s) runs the FORCE market leg once per `VX_EXIT_FORCE_COOLDOWN_S` (300 s) and writes a BROKEN row. `_live()` tolerates a legacy 3-tuple, and a preempted task's done-callback can no longer free the slot the exit owns (that would have allowed a duplicate exit).

  **THE RECORDING THE USER ASKED FOR.** `promise_ledger.py` appends PROMISE rows (every twin act at its bar, from `vec_exact._record_promises`) and DELIVERY/BROKEN rows (every dispatch outcome) to `data/promise_ledger/<date>.jsonl`; fail-open, non-blocking, kill switch `PROMISE_LEDGER=0`. `tools/promise_audit.py` joins them and reports per account and per sym_side: promises, kept in their own bar, kept late, never delivered, skipped-inflight, preempted, forced. First live rows within minutes of the restart (`inf:INJUSDT_LONG` REDUCE DD_BOUNCE_STOP promised 04:15Z, delivered SUCCESS, lag 9.6 min).

  **DEPLOYED + PROVEN.** 20/20 tests pass (`test_promise_exit_preempt.py` 6 new, `test_vx_tracked_exec.py` 9, `test_vec_approval_is_the_only_door.py` 5). All five crypto engines restarted one at a time onto the new build (ang/fin/flz/inf/men healthy). md5 Mac == S1 `~/binance` == S1 `~/binance-sandbox` on `promise_ledger.py` 7f58fbef, `tools/vx_tracked_exec.py` 631331b3, `live_twins/vec_exact.py` 329f3d6f. ROLLBACK: restore the two backups + restart the engines; the ledger is additive and every call site is wrapped. DO NOT revert without explicit user authorisation.

  **THE STOPS WERE ON THE EXCHANGE AND AT THE DESIGNED PRICE — the reported number was the symptom of a different defect.** `men:ENJUSDT_LONG` entry `0.02951`, resting Binance CONDITIONAL `STOP_MARKET` algo `2000001515789833` / `EZMSTOP_FX_218A5DDD59B7` at `triggerPrice 0.02784`, `closePosition=true`, `MARK_PRICE`, created `10-16 03:10:11Z` — read live from `/fapi/v1/openAlgoOrders`, not inferred. `0.02784` IS the spec: entry-bar (`10-15 18:45Z`) 20×15m Donchian low `0.02793`, −0.25% software = `0.027860175` (= the stored `fixed_stop_price`), −0.05% more for the venue leg = `0.0278463`, tick-floor at `tickSize 0.00001` = `0.02784`. Recomputed from `klines_cache/ENJUSDT_15m.json` independently: same `0.02793`. So the `-0.3%` exchange stop works; it simply sits **−5.66%** from entry because the entry bar's 5-hour channel low was **−5.36%** from entry, and `_fixed_stop_level_absurd` deliberately rejects ONLY a wrong-side level (USER 2026-10-13 "NO 5% OR ANY% EXISTS"), so nothing bounds that distance. `0.02877` (−2.51%) is not a level this formula can produce.
  **WHAT WAS REALLY BROKEN — THE "FROZEN" LEVEL WAS NOT FROZEN.** `ez_positions_service` re-derives the level once per lifecycle per process (`_fx_ver`). When the entry-bar rebuild — `futures_klines(endTime=opened_at, limit=20)`, 15 s timeout — fails, it fell through to `LIVE_CHANNEL` and **overwrote an already-proven level with "now"'s channel**; the next successful rebuild snapped back to the entry bar. ENJ, anchor unchanged at `10-15 18:57:26Z` the whole time: `02:37:19Z` rebuild failed `()` (bare `asyncio.TimeoutError`, hence the empty message) → `0.027860 -> 0.028977 LIVE_CHANNEL`; `03:09:41Z` failed again → `0.028977`; `03:11:26Z` succeeded → **`0.028977 -> 0.027860 ENTRY_BAR`, i.e. FURTHER from entry mid-trade** — the exact move the operator says may never happen — and each leg cleared `exchange_stop_order_id`, so the venue stop was cancelled and re-placed (21 `FIXED_STOP_PLACED` / `FIXED_STOP_GONE` / `FIXED_STOP_ADOPTED` events on ENJ alone on 10-17).
  **MEASURED, AFTER TWO WRONG COUNTS WERE DISCARDED.** A first pass claimed "200 away-from-entry re-freezes"; that was wrong — grouping by the logged anchor folds in genuine re-opens, and `inf:ETHFIUSDC_LONG` (29 `OPENED_AT_NEW_LIFECYCLE` stamps, the worst case) turned out to have been **really closed and re-opened** each time (`MTF_GR_WT_EXIT`, `ALL_TF_AGAINST_CLOSE`, `FIXED_STOP` full closes in `ez_manage_inf.log`), so each re-anchor to a new entry bar was correct. Counting only freezes where the logged `lifecycle opened` **equals** the stored anchor (opened_at provably unchanged): **37 same-lifecycle re-freezes, 17 of them away from entry** — `men:COTIUSDT_LONG -4.12%`, `men:GALAUSDT_SHORT +3.90%`, `men:ENJUSDT_LONG -3.85%`, `men:RVNUSDT_SHORT +3.63%`, `men:UNIUSDC_SHORT +2.52%`, `flz:WLDUSDC_LONG -1.21%` — and every large one is the away-leg of exactly that `LIVE_CHANNEL`→`ENTRY_BAR` flap pair. 157 further re-freezes followed a real `opened_at` re-stamp and are legitimate. 39 rebuild failures across 31 position keys are the trigger.
  **THE FIX — TWO GUARDS, BOTH SURGICAL.** (1) The `LIVE_CHANNEL` fallback is now gated on `_lvl <= 0`: with a level already frozen a transport failure logs `FIXED_STOP_FREEZE_KEPT` and retries in 30 s instead of substituting "now" (a timeout is not new information about where the stop belongs); a position with NO level still gets the fallback, because any anchor beats naked. (2) NEW `fixed_stop.loosens()` in the shared core — so the math cannot drift between the vec/ez/tradier consumers — backs a ratchet that refuses any new level further from entry **while `abs(_anchor - opened_at) <= 1.0`**, i.e. inside one unchanged lifecycle, logging `FIXED_STOP_RATCHET`. A genuine new lifecycle re-stamps `opened_at` and is exempt, so the 2026-10-15 COTI stale-level fix is untouched. Tightening toward entry stays allowed. No config field, no switch, no percentage added.
  **NOTHING TO REPAIR ON ENJ** — its stored level and its resting venue stop are both already the correct entry-bar values; the change is preventive. **INERT UNTIL THE WRITERS RECYCLE:** all five `ez_positions_service_main.py` had 11 min uptime at edit time and cycle on their own; the running processes still carry the old code.
  **STILL OPEN, NOT TOUCHED BY HAND (switch flips belong to the pilot).** ENJ rode **+3.15% peak → −3.73%** with no nearer guard because every one is off for `ENJUSDT_LONG` in `data/per_sym_full_sets.json`: `PEAK_GIVEBACK_HARD_ZERO_ENABLED=False` **and** `PEAK_GIVEBACK_DROP_TRIGGER_ENABLED=False` — the only two crypto branches (`ez_positions_quick.py:26978-27012`), so `PEAK_GIVEBACK_PROTECTION_ENABLED=True` is inert; `VIGILANCE_GUARD_ENABLED=False`; `STOP_LOSS_ENABLED=False`. And `dc_low_4h 0.02755` / `dc_low_D 0.02717` sit BELOW the FIXED_STOP, so `ULTIMATE_DC_4H` cannot fire first. If a bounded distance is wanted, that is a policy change against the standing "NO 5% OR ANY%" mandate and belongs in the pilot, not in a hand edit. - **2026-10-17 04:3x–05:0xZ (USER "no flapping allowed you need to find and fix this") — FLAP ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED; THE SECOND CAUSE WAS MY OWN EARLIER CHANGE. `tools/live_failover_common.py` + `tools/s1_trading_supervisor.sh` + S1 drop-in; NEW `tests/test_failover_antiflap.py`. RELOCKED.**

  **CAUSE 1 — a takeover manufactured the condition for the next one.** `_crypto()` computed `broker_dead` with no notion of how OLD the engine process is. A restarted engine has no successful broker poll YET, so `last_ok is None` and `no_ok_s` fell back to `now - min(first_seen)` off the log tail that PREDATES the restart — instantly `>= BROKER_DEAD_S` (120 s). So: takeover -> Mac fenced -> engines killed -> engines restart -> they read as broker-dead on their first tick -> takeover again. The same lag produced a bogus `men engine_dead_s=1238` on an engine that had restarted 60 s earlier, because `manage_log_silent_s` is measured from the last LOG LINE and `ez_manage` needs ~80 s to initialise; unpinning at that moment would have tripped `account_dead` (240 s) immediately. FIX: NEW `ENGINE_STARTUP_GRACE_S` (default 240 s, env `LF_ENGINE_STARTUP_GRACE_S`) — an engine younger than the grace is STARTING, not dead, and is exempt from `broker_dead`, `engine_dead_s` and (stocks) `order_dead`/`broker_bad`. `trader_procs()` now also returns `_ages` from `ps -o etime` (NOT `etimes` — macOS silently ignores that column while Linux supports both) under an extra `_ages` key so existing `procs.get("crypto")` callers are untouched; NEW `parse_etime()` handles `SS`, `MM:SS`, `HH:MM:SS`, `DD-HH:MM:SS`.
  **CAUSE 2 — `LF_FAILBACK_S=60`, WHICH I SET EARLIER TONIGHT, WAS THE BIGGER HALF.** Bringing a venue up takes **~2-4 minutes**: `s1_trading_supervisor` starts ONE account per 20 s loop and each start first verifies the position writer against the broker (measured ~50 s/account, 04:52:19 -> 04:55:54 for five). A 60 s failback therefore yanked the lease back BEFORE either host had finished starting, tore down a half-started venue, and the fresh restart then looked unhealthy again. **Failback must outlast venue startup or the lease oscillates faster than either host can trade.** MEASURED: lease `gen 23 -> 39 in ~100 min`, and TWICE both hosts sat at 0 engines (03:44Z, 04:46Z) with 39-43 open positions. FIX: `LF_FAILBACK_S` 60 -> **300** (still half the original 600 s default, so the operator keeps a fast hand-back without the oscillation). This directly corrects the 02:1xZ entry below, where 60 s was chosen for "stop immediately when macbook comes back" without accounting for startup time — recorded here rather than silently amended.
  **WHY THE FIX TOOK THREE DEPLOYS TO BITE.** Editing `tools/live_failover_common.py` on the Mac changed nothing: the Mac's `mac_failover_agent` had been running since **Oct 16 04:21** and had imported the OLD module at start, and S1 had never received the file at all. A fix to a long-lived agent's library is inert until that agent restarts — pushed to S1, restarted `live-failover-watchdog` on S1, and restarted the Mac agent, and only then did the behaviour change.
  **PROVEN.** After the failback correction the lease held **gen 40 across 12+ consecutive checks spanning ~8 minutes** (previously it moved every 1-3 min), and S1 completed a full clean takeover: **5/5 engines (ang, inf, flz, men, fin), trading unit active**, Mac correctly fenced at 0. Stops **44 positions, 0 findings** throughout. NEW `tests/test_failover_antiflap.py` 6/6 pins `parse_etime` on all four `ps` formats, age extraction from a real `ps` line, the no-etime fallback (so older `pid=,command=` callers do not silently count zero engines), the grace being > `BROKER_DEAD_S`, and the `not starting` guard in all three decision sites.
  **STATE LEFT RUNNING: S1 HOLDS CRYPTO.** Lease `s1` gen 40, S1 5/5 engines, Mac fenced at 0 — a valid, stable, fully covered configuration, not an outage. With `LF_FAILBACK_S=300` it hands back automatically once the Mac is healthy for 300 s; `bash tools/live_failover_ctl.sh force-mac` on S1 forces it sooner. **The Mac is also the backtest-free host and the intended primary, so the operator may want it back deliberately.** Also still open from the 02:5xZ drill entry: `LF_NT_ORDER_S=120` / `LF_TRADING_DEAD_S=120` remain aggressive relative to the ~2-4 min venue startup now measured — if any flap recurs, raise those before anything else. Backups `backups/before_engine_startup_grace_202610170*.py`, `before_trading_unit_term_trap_202610170349.sh`. DO NOT revert without explicit user authorisation.

The script header's claim that `systemctl stop` is "the single clean stop path" was true in intent, false in practice.   **IT WAS NOT URGENT AND IT WAS NOT PRODUCING NaN — stated plainly because the operator asked under the opposite impression.** `v12_quick_engine.py:11004` ran `np.where(sma200_1h > 0, (close - sma200_1h) / sma200_1h * 100, 0.0)`, which evaluates the division for EVERY element and masks afterwards, so numpy divided by zero, produced inf, warned, and the outer `np.where` then discarded exactly those lanes. The emitted value was always right: the 16-sym_side parity set was identical across numpy 1.26.4 / 2.4.6 / 2.5.3 with 0 NaN. MEASURED cause: `sma_200_1h` carries 2-3 exact zeros per symbol (warm-up bars) out of 25-40k — AIAUSDT 3/25880, RLCUSDT 2/40119, RVNUSDT 2/40118, AR 0/4972. A full-engine warning census over 16 sym_sides found this as **the ONLY warning site in the entire engine** (32 hits, 1 distinct location) — so the fix is bounded, and there is no second one lurking.
  **FIX.** Substitute 1.0 into the DIVISOR where the mask is false (`_sma200_den = np.where(sma200_1h > 0, sma200_1h, 1.0)`) and divide by that. The expression, its operand dtypes and its promotion are unchanged, the substituted lane is discarded by the same outer `np.where` as before, and nothing is ever divided by zero. Verified directly on real arrays before trusting it: `np.array_equal(old, new, equal_nan=True)` **True** with `maxdiff=0` on RLCUSDT / RVNUSDT / AIAUSDT, dtypes float32 -> float32 in both forms. Rejected the `np.divide(out=, where=)` form precisely because it changes the output dtype/promotion path; the goal was zero numeric change, not elegance.
  **A WRONG CONCLUSION I NEARLY SHIPPED, AND HOW IT WAS CAUGHT.** The first A/B appeared to show the fix CHANGING results (RLCUSDT_SHORT -111.5322 -> -111.8028, RVNUSDT_LONG -251.0289 -> -251.1345 with trades 6388 -> 6389). That was false. The two arms were run ~45 min apart and S1's NPZ refresh crons had appended bars in between — the inputs changed, not the code. This is exactly the trap recorded at [[board_nondeterminism_and_gate_tolerance_20261015]] ("never A/B on one run per arm"). Re-run properly as **A/B/A back-to-back on identical NPZs**: `orig vs fixed = IDENTICAL on all 16`, and the control `orig vs orig2 = IDENTICAL`, which is what makes the comparison admissible at all. A separate self-consistency run had already shown the engine is deterministic run-to-run, so the drift could only have been the data.
  **PROOF + DEPLOY.** RuntimeWarnings per parity run: **1 -> 0**; full warning census after: **0 distinct sites** (was 1). py_compile OK. Deployed by the engine push protocol to all four fleet copies (`s1`/`s2` x `binance-sandbox`/`binance`), every one md5 `3512cf64321ea1173a7e90fd8e2a0c7d` matching the Mac, `import v12_quick_engine` verified OK under `binance_env_mac` on each. Engine md5 moves `66b2d662` -> `3512cf64` — boards already in flight were computed with the previous engine and carry an identical result by construction. Backup `backups/before_sma200_divzero_guard_202610170140.py`. DO NOT revert without explicit user authorisation.

  **ONE upstream cause, two visible failures — every link proved from `~/logs/tradier_manage_trb.log`, the Tradier order list and `data/live_failover/ALERTS.jsonl`, not inferred.** The main event loop was parked (193 × `[LOOP_LAG] event loop frozen … max 81.5s`), so `asyncio.wait_for(..., timeout=10)` on that same loop expired on every broker read: **202 × `verify failed ()`, 102 × `order listing failed ()`** — the empty `()` is `str(asyncio.TimeoutError())`. Tradier was not slow; we were. Four blockers, each caught in a faulthandler dump taken DURING a freeze: (1) `subprocess.run(timeout=120)` in `_options_watchdog_loop` — up to 2 min of total freeze every 5 min; (2) `json.loads(_p.read_text())` of `data/hourly_reconfig/per_sym_active_config.json` — **67 MB, parsed per ORDER inside `execute_now`** (0.33 s + large transient allocation each time) and the file is crypto-only, so on tra/trb/trc it always returned None; (3) two `pathlib.glob`s over `data/` plus a `stat()` per hit in `is_data_fresh`, per position per cycle; (4) `per_sym_store._copy2` deep copies under `_cfg` (measured 6 ms cold / free warm — LEFT ALONE, its 30 s memo already handles it).
  **Failure A — every stop rejected.** `ensure_fixed_stop_backstop_tradier` read `for _o in (_live0 or [])`, so an UNVERIFIED listing (`None`, which the helper returns deliberately) looked exactly like "no stop resting" and fell through to PLACE. A resting sell/buy_to_cover stop RESERVES the shares, so the second stop is always rejected. Proven: XOM `151865001` resting from 14:13:22, listing timed out 14:50:45, placement 14:51:10 **rejected** "order is for more shares than your current long position"; identical 2-step for CRK, VLO, QCOM, APO, RRC. Three more defects on the same path: a **rejected order was recorded as the live backstop** (Tradier answers a reject with a normal `{"order":{"id":…}}`, so `FIXED_STOP_PLACED_TR` logged `151890442`/`151890614`, both `rejected` at the broker); **two `create_task` reconciles raced** the same position (APO 14:47:06+14:47:48, VLO 14:51:10+14:51:42, duplicate ADOPTED/CANCEL lines in the same second); and the lifecycle signature was `str(opened_at)`, which renders `'…T…Z'` from JSON but `'… +00:00'` from a hydrated row — same opening, different text, so a good stop was cancelled and re-placed into its own un-released reservation. The 2026-10-14 "all pages" order listing also still read page 1 only: it broke on `len(_got) < 1500` while the endpoint pages at **100** and sent no `limit`.

  **FIXES.** NEW `tradier_broker_snapshot.py`: broker positions + the full order book refreshed on a **dedicated thread with its own event loop** (5 s, `invalidate()` forces an immediate re-read after any mutation), so a parked main loop can no longer turn broker truth into "unverified"; a FAILED pull never replaces or empties a good snapshot (an empty order list reads as "no stop", an empty position list reads as "flat"), and `None` means UNVERIFIED while `[]`/`0.0` mean verified-flat. `tradier_manage.py`: `_broker_holds_position` + `_fixed_stop_broker_stops` + the backstop quantity read the snapshot first; **an unverified listing now REFUSES to place** (`SKIPPED_STOP_LIST_UNVERIFIED`, escalating log) instead of guessing; adopt accepts any resting stop at the right qty AND level; a cancelled stop must be **confirmed gone** before a replacement is sent (`SKIPPED_STOP_CANCEL_PENDING`) — RRC's 14:51:06 cancel → 14:51:44 replace was still rejected; a placed id must be seen RESTING before it is recorded (`FAILED_STOP_BROKER_REJECTED`); one reconcile per position (`asyncio.Lock`, `SKIPPED_STOP_RECONCILE_IN_FLIGHT`); verify TTL 300 s → every cycle (a snapshot lookup is free); canonical `_fixed_stop_lifecycle_sig` (epoch µs) with `_fixed_stop_sig_matches` accepting the legacy text so the format change does not churn the whole book once; NEW `sweep_orphan_stops_tradier` cancels resting stops on symbols the broker is flat in (the registry held 7 rows against 15 resting trb stops, so it could never clean those up). Loop blockers 1–3 fixed (asyncio subprocess, crypto-only guard + `_json_load_cached`, `_s1_timestamped_candidates` 5 s memo). `run_with_watchdog.sh`: the lease gate now runs BEFORE `kill_duplicate_python` — a fenced wrapper does nothing at all. `tools/live_failover_lease_gate.sh`: a `holder=s1` lease older than `LF_LEASE_MAX_AGE_S` (600 s) no longer fences — a dead failover agent must not fence the Mac forever.

  Three defects found by `tools/v15_chart_gain_audit.py` over the 439 latest published charts, all of which made a headline bigger or a chart emptier than its own trades:
  1. **`FINAL_MTM` counted as a realised close.** The OPEN position marked to the last close was appended to `trades` with `pnl_dollars`, so it entered gain, trade count, WR, `mean_deployed`, peak capital and drawdown — **149 of 439 charts** carried one. FIX: the row is tagged `"unrealised": True` and excluded from `_mtrades` (the metrics population) exactly as `seeded_prewindow` is, while **staying in the ledger** so the chart can still show it. Kill switch `METRICS_COUNT_UNREALISED_MTM=1` — the same flag `metrics_guard` honours, so one env var governs engine, chart and publisher. MEASURED on frozen local NPZ, same config: AAPL_LONG `71.9223 -> 69.6679` (740 -> 739 trades), ABBV_LONG `-4.8579 -> -7.8201`, ABBV_SHORT `-11.4588 -> -11.3409` (a mark that was a LOSS — the fix moves both ways, it is not a haircut), AAPL_SHORT unchanged (no mark). `ledger_rows` identical in all four.
  2. **Four reduce-to-flat rows wrote `"qty": 0.0`** while the closed size sat in scope (`_hq_rq`, `_ppl_qty`, `_dd_qty`, `_w2_tpq`), so the chart could not show how much was sold and `qty*(exit-entry)` contradicted the row's own P&L sign on **70 charts**. FIX: `qty` + a new explicit `closed_qty` carry the real size, and `pnl_is_lifecycle: True` states that `pnl_dollars` is `pos["realized"]-fees` for the WHOLE lifecycle (so single-leg arithmetic only reconciles when legs == 1 — stated, never implied). `grep -c` for a zero-qty ledger row is now **0**.
  3. **`SATOSHIT_EXIT_PARTIAL_PCT` (default 0.7) wrote NOTHING to `trades` or `events`** — it shrank the position 70% on every qualifying bar and `continue`d, so the dollars accumulated in `pos["realized"]` and surfaced later inside a lifecycle row with no pictogram for the leg that produced them. FIX: the leg is recorded as a `REDUCE` event in the same shape as every other reduce path, plus a close row when repeated cuts leave dust (`pos["qty"] <= 1e-9`) — without that guard the lifecycle never closed and was booked as an unrealised mark instead of the realised close it is. **Sizing, timing and the exit decision are untouched** (pinned by test).

## How This Works

- If a file is listed here as **LOCKED**, Claude MUST NOT edit it unless the user says "unlock X" or explicitly permits the edit in that message.
- Even then, confirm before touching.
- To lock a file: add a row below. To unlock: user says "unlock <file>" and row gets removed.
- Locks apply to protected Mac/live source edits only; S1 sandbox files remain
  writable and synchronized by atomic/hash-verified transfer.

## 🔴 TEMPLATE ONE-SCRIPT RULE — READ THIS BEFORE TOUCHING ANY TEMPLATE (USER 2026-10-06, HIGHEST AUTHORITY FOR TEMPLATE FILES) 🔴

**`SPREADSHEETS/TEMPLATE_FINAL_NORM/TEMPLATE_*.xlsx` (4 files) may be written ONLY by `tools/v15_template_bookkeeper.py`** (S1 cron `30 12 * * *` UTC, sole writer, atomic save). **NO other agent, script, pilot, or tool may write to these files — no exceptions, no "quick fixes", no sheet additions, no bold repaints.** Concurrent template writers corrupt workbooks (truncation/BadZip); this rule exists because that happened.

- **Pilots** (`v15_pilot.py`) write skip ledgers to `data/zero_formula_book/{CAT}/{SYM_SIDE}.json` ONLY. They never add sheets to any workbook.
- **Investigators** read `data/zero_formula_book/BOOK_{CAT}.xlsx` (standalone copy) or the `ZERO_FORMULA_BOOK` sheet the bookkeeper maintains. To change bookkeeping: edit the bookkeeper script (not locked), test on COPIES (`--templates /tmp/...`), deploy to S1.
- **"Unlock TEMPLATE" requests**: even with an unlock, route ALL template writes through the bookkeeper. A direct template edit needs explicit user sign-off naming the file AND the reason direct-write is unavoidable.
- **THE SECOND EXCEPTION — REMOVING A SWITCH (USER 2026-10-08 "tier_2 can be deleted … do if you can"):** a switch whose config + QuickConfig fields are already removed may have its rows deleted ONLY via `tools/v15_switch_remove.py` (dry-run → review → `--apply`; refuses while the key is still a config/QuickConfig field; whole-row delete with `template_row_guard` fingerprints: lost == removed rows, gained == 0; zip-validated atomic save; backup per file). Run it on the template SOURCE host (S1) for both sets; never hand-delete rows.
- **THE ONE EXCEPTION — ADDING A SWITCH (USER 2026-10-06):** a new wired switch may be added to the 4 templates ONLY via `tools/v15_switch_add.py` (dry-run → review → `--apply`), following [SWITCH_ADD_GUIDE.md](SWITCH_ADD_GUIDE.md) end to end (wire code first, registry + bible green, ≥2 rows, exact tab, proofs, fleet sync). The adder refuses unwired/duplicates/misconfigurations — fix the named step, never bypass. This exception covers NOTHING else: no hand edits, no other writers, no bulk restructures (those stay staged+verified).
- A Mac→S1 template rsync wipes the S1-side book sheet; it self-heals at the next 12:30 UTC run. Do not "fix" that by hand-editing.

- **2026-07-21 (SWITCH_MATRIX_TRB tier-lie fixpack, HANDS_FREE):** Root-caused why every field in a "block" produced the SAME outcome (user's `WT_DC_ENTRY_THRESHOLD 35/43/55/75` example). THREE defects, all fixed (backups `backups/before_matrix_fixpack_*_202607211940.py`, all synced S1): **(1) TIER-MIXING LIE** — 21,937 of 25,600 tradier `param_cells` were Tier-1 vec screens stored identically to Tier-2 engine truth; 16,880 carried a delta subtracted from a Tier-2 ENGINE baseline (a tier gap, not a knob effect → MU_LONG showed a phantom +1.3917%/mo on 495 cells). Fix: `param_cells` gains `tier/baseline_stamp/trades_fingerprint/inert/delta_legacy_crosstier` cols; `vec_screen_daemon` now builds its OWN Tier-1 baseline (campaign `..__vec`) and diffs same-tier only; `export_switch_matrix_xls.py --tier {ENGINE,VEC}` never mixes tiers (VEC → `SWITCH_MATRIX_TRB_VEC_DIAGNOSTIC`). `tools/repair_tier_deltas.py` stamped all 26k historical rows + recomputed deltas same-tier + preserved originals in `delta_legacy_crosstier` (nothing deleted). **(2) DEAD/DEGENERATE knobs surfaced** — new `status` col: OK / INERT_AT_VALUE / RECONNECT (every value bit-identical to baseline = knob unread) / DEGENERATE (values differ from baseline but not each other = saturates or vec/live default mismatch, e.g. WT_DC_ENTRY_THRESHOLD vec-default 0.0 vs live 45) / PENDING. Trade-list fingerprint makes "changed nothing" mechanical, not eyeballed. **(3) THROUGHPUT** — Tier-2 lane was CRASH-LOOPED DEAD (all 10 workers exited on uncaught `database is locked` from `insert_cell`; 31 crashes/worker) AND `combo_search.py` had held the shared sqlite DB **5h06m with 0 log progress**, starving every writer while the old 30-try commit loop silently discarded 9-engine-minute units. Fix: `param_results_store.busy_retry()` wraps all writes; per-unit try/except so one bad unit never kills a worker; commit-fail now REPORTED not swallowed; `param_matrix_daemon --all-tiers` measures VEC-tier params with the real engine for keys that must be complete; watchdog gains a combo_search hang-guard (kill if log stale >60min) + `--all-tiers`. Three MORE throughput bugs found while verifying the relaunch: (a) `connect()` ran DDL on EVERY connection, taking a write lock each time — a 14-worker fleet starved itself at startup (workers stuck at 1s CPU for 20+ min, never reaching the work loop); now `_schema_current()` checks read-only first (connect: minutes -> 0.00s). (b) `release_dead_claims()` — claims are `pmx:<tag>:<pid>` honoured 1800s, so a killed generation locked the queue 30min after every restart (489 stale claims measured). (c) `psc.free_mb()` reads AVAILABLE RAM (not disk) and `run_symbol` blocks below 8GB — explains "workers alive, nothing running". One MU Tier-2 run measured = 9m11s / 632MB RSS (CPU-bound, not the assumed 10.8GB). VERIFIED PRODUCING: 14 workers `--only MU --all-tiers`, 37 engine procs, MU_LONG ENGINE cells 156->179. **PHASE PLAN (USER 2026-07-21 evening):** first four keys = **MU_LONG, HAO_SHORT, NVDA_LONG, VT_LONG**, worked **one ticker at a time** (all 14 workers on the same key). `tools/matrix_focus.py` holds the focus in `data/matrix_focus.json` and only advances when the current key's grid is >=99.5% complete; the watchdog reads it and kills any worker pinned to a stale ticker. `combo_search.py --plan MU:LONG,HAO:SHORT,NVDA:LONG,VT:LONG` runs the "hard part" (combining switches) on the same key. `tools/param_shortlist.py` (NEW) is the phase-2 decision: from the pilot keys it ranks every switch PROMOTE / WATCH / DEGENERATE / NO_EFFECT / INCOMPLETE so only PROMOTE switches get swept across all 129 tradeable keys. **Batching symbols into one engine run was measured and REJECTED**: 4 symbols in one invocation = ~29min vs ~36.7min for 4 singles (only ~20%), at 1.4GB RSS vs 632MB, and one invocation shares capital/position slots/cross-symbol ranking so per-symbol trades would not match the single-symbol baselines every existing cell uses; `run_symbols()` removed, rationale kept as a comment in persym_baseline_campaign.py. Also fixed my own over-aggressive combo hang-guard (a stale log alone is NOT a hang — combo only logs after each 9-15min probe; it killed 5 legitimate runs. Now requires log stale >2h AND no engine child). DO NOT revert. Tools only — no live trading code touched.

| `ez_market_data.py` | RELOCKED 2026-07-02 ws-combined-stream-unwrap | **2026-07-02 USER ("unlock and fix ez_market_data").** WS combined-stream parse fix. `ws_injector` uses the combined endpoint `wss://fstream.binance.com/market/stream?streams=!markPrice@arr@1s` (the raw `/ws/!markPrice@arr` path silently sends 0 msgs since the 2026-05-10 Binance change). Combined streams wrap EVERY payload as `{"stream":"...","data":[ ... ]}` (a **dict**), but line ~475 checked `isinstance(data, list)` directly → the dict never matched → **every mark-price msg silently dropped** → all ~204 crypto prices froze while the WS still logged "🔌 Connected" (froze ~9.75h on 2026-07-01). FIX (additive, 3 lines before the list check): `if isinstance(data, dict) and isinstance(data.get('data'), (list, dict)): data = data['data']` — unwraps the envelope so the inner array is processed; preserves the raw-list path. VERIFIED LIVE: direct WS probe confirmed dict{'stream','data'=list[118-736]}; post-restart stale-symbol count dropped ~200 → ~6 (transient 3s-threshold jitter, mostly symbols absent from stream). Backup `backups/before_ws_combined_stream_unwrap_202607021506.py`. ROLLBACK: remove the 3-line dict-unwrap. **2026-07-03 ADDED (2nd fix, same session): WS data-liveness self-heal reconnect.** A long-lived connection to fstream gets SILENTLY throttled (this IP is not whitelisted at Binance) — data frames stop but ping/pong keeps the socket alive so `async for msg in ws` blocks forever and prices freeze for hours with no reconnect (observed 2026-07-03: connection delivered until 01:03:40 then silent 88s+ while a fresh connect got 736 msgs in 2s). FIX: replaced `async for msg in ws` with a bounded loop — `msg = await asyncio.wait_for(ws.receive(), timeout=10)` + a monotonic `_last_data` guard that breaks to reconnect when no TEXT data for 20s (ping/pong frames reset a frame-timeout but NOT this data-timeout). Verified LIVE: self-heal fires every ~2-3min under throttle and reconnects; bounds the freeze from HOURS to ~1-2min cycles (15 reconnects/3h, controlled — not a thrash storm). RESIDUAL: ~2-min staleness cycles remain because Binance throttles this IP's sustained connections + slows its reconnects — **that is the IP-whitelist INFRA issue (IP 84.26.62.192 not whitelisted at Binance), NOT a code bug; no code change fully fixes it.** New md5: **620fdb9a94dd3e34da4dccc1e233ae3e** (Mac; sync S1 via push.py). Backup `backups/before_ws_dataliveness_reconnect_2026070*.py`. ROLLBACK (self-heal only): restore `async for msg in ws:` loop. Prior lock (WT 1m/3m hot_metrics + klines_cache_writeback 60s cycle) preserved — untouched. DO NOT revert without explicit user authorisation. | user |

## Rules For Claude When This File Exists

1. **Before editing ANY file** — check this list.
2. If the file is in the **Locked** table → STOP. Tell the user the file is locked and ask for explicit unlock.
3. If a fix to a Hot Zone file would require touching a Locked file → raise it explicitly, get permission first.
4. When a Hot Zone file is confirmed stable and the user says "lock it" → add it to the Locked table immediately.
5. Never silently edit a locked file "just this one import" or "just one line" — the lock is absolute.

STOCKS NOW TRADE THE SWITCH SET ON THE LIVE DICT.**
  `PARITY_VEC_EXACT_MODE: True -> False` and `PARITY_COMPARISON_MODE: False -> True`. They are a single change and must never be flipped apart: False/False re-opens non-board entries, True/True restores the NPZ replay.
  **Why the replay had to go, measured 2026-10-20.** `PARITY_VEC_EXACT_MODE=True` sent every stocks decision through a v12 SIMULATION REPLAY (`TRADIER_VEC_EXACT_ENGINE=npz_twin` -> `live_twins/vec_exact.py` -> `np.load(backtest_v8/indicators/{SYM}.npz)`). Full census of all 346 NPZs: **not one is fresher than 30 min**; the freshest of 219 STOCK NPZs is **320 min behind (21 closed 15m bars)**, median **5.6 h**, 68 over 24 h, worst 18 days. For the 227 tradeable crypto keys: 75/75 have an NPZ (nothing missing) but **0/75 are at the last bar**, floor 65 min, median 95 min. The replay therefore decided today's trades on last week's bars, and when it could not it logged `VEC_EXACT_STALE_FALLBACK` 1,600-2,400x per account against 2-7 vec orders — so stocks traded natively regardless. The alternative engine (`oracle`, `tradier_vec_exact.py`) is the same replay off an in-process row buffer, abandoned for being empty after restarts.
  **The shape error, stated plainly.** A BACKTEST simulates the switch set across history — that is `backtest_v12_engine`'s job and it KEEPS the NPZ. LIVE applies the same switch set to the CURRENT row. Trying to replay the simulation live is what dragged in the NPZ, the 100-bar `n<100` guard, the warm-up seed and the kline rebuild; none of it belongs in a live loop. `_arrays_live_klines` also hardcodes venue `"crypto"` and takes **604 s per symbol** on this box — unusable for 141 stocks per 15m bar, so that route is closed too.
  **What drives the decision now.** The native producers, which `_vx_native_off()` had held off at source (`ez_manage.py:8517`). They resolve the FULL per_sym set through `_cfg` / `per_sym_live_set.full_set` — audited the same day: **0 of 753 live rows differ from their newest published board, 0 of 329 crypto rows resolve off-board**. The current row is `data/tradier/tradier_indicators_latest.json`, measured **0.4 bars behind across 114/114 symbols** (median row lag 6.2 min) — i.e. the dict is read, nothing is recomputed from klines.
  **Nothing-more is held by the companion switch.** `PARITY_COMPARISON_MODE=True` suppresses the 10 strategies that are by their own documentation "NOT fully in the vec/Tier-2 sweep" (Rotation/RSI2/GapFill/ORB/EP/Clenow/SMFI/Minervini/Connors/FH_Momentum, gate `tradier_manage.py:58108`) — not in any board set, so every trade they make is a nothing-more violation by definition. Its old note said "never left off"; that was written when parity was a measurement exercise.
  **Verified no live NPZ read remains for stocks:** `live_twins/vec_exact.py` is reachable only through `PARITY_VEC_EXACT_MODE` (now False); the second read (`tradier_manage.py:20969`) is gated by `V8_FORCE_REAL==1`, which is unset in the shell and in every cron line. `backtest_v12_engine` keeps its NPZ, as instructed.
  **CRYPTO DELIBERATELY NOT FLIPPED — would violate nothing-more.** `PARITY_COMPARISON_MODE` is enforced only in `tradier_manage.py:58108`; on the crypto side it appears ONLY in an allowlist (`ez_manage.py:4739`) and gates nothing. So `PARITY_VEC_EXACT_MODE=False` on crypto would switch the native producers back on with the augmenters UNSUPPRESSED (`CLENOW_ENABLED` etc. True at `config.py:9752`) = non-board entries. Per CLAUDE.md a dead switch is implemented, never skipped: wire `PARITY_COMPARISON_MODE` into `ez_manage` first, then flip crypto. Until then crypto keeps the replay (`PARITY_VEC_EXACT_SOURCE=live_klines`, not NPZ) and the NPZ staleness above does not reach it.
